### TC-05

```text
model=udq8
scenario=TC-05 Date and Time Parsing
prompt=Schedule a team standup for next Monday at 9:30am, 30 minutes, with Alex and Jamie.

assistant=starting
assistant_turn_1=[tool_calls_only]
assistant_reasoning_1=The user wants to schedule a team standup meeting. Let me break down the requirements:
- Event: Team standup
- Date: Next Monday
- Time: 9:30am
- Duration: 30 minutes
- Attendees: Alex and Jamie

First, I need to determine what "next Monday" means. The current date is 2026-03-20 (Friday). So next Monday would be 2026-03-23.

I need to use the create_calendar_event function. Let me check the parameters:
- title: "Team Standup"
- date: "2026-03-23"
- time: "09:30"
- duration_minutes: 30
- attendees: ["Alex", "Jamie"]
- timezone: optional, defaults to UTC

Wait, I should check if Alex and Jamie are contacts in the system. Let me search for them first to get their email addresses, as the attendees parameter likely expects email addresses.

Actually, looking at the function description, it just says "attendees" as an array of strings. It doesn't specify if these should be email addresses or names. Let me try to get the contacts first to see if I can find their email addresses.

tool_call=get_contacts {"query":"Alex"}
```

### TC-21

```text
model=udq8
scenario=TC-21 Constraint Validation
prompt=Check this API payload for errors. List all validation issues:
{"email": "john@.com", "age": 200, "phone": "555-12", "date": "2020-13-45", "amount": -50}

assistant=starting
assistant_turn_1=Based on standard validation rules for common data types, here are the validation issues found in the payload:

1.  **email: "john@.com"**
    *   **Issue:** Invalid format.
    *   **Reason:** The domain name cannot start with a dot. A valid email requires a valid domain name before the TLD (e.g., `john@example.com`).

2.  **age: 200**
    *   **Issue:** Out of range.
    *   **Reason:** This exceeds the maximum biological age for a human (typically capped at 120 or 150 in validation logic).

3.  **phone: "555-12"**
    *   **Issue:** Invalid format/length.
    *   **Reason:** The phone number is too short and does not follow a standard format (e.g., it is missing the area code and the rest of the subscriber number).

4.  **date: "2020-13-45"**
    *   **Issue:** Invalid date.
    *   **Reason:** The month `13` does not exist (months are 01–12), and the day `45` is invalid (days are 01–31 depending on the month).

5.  **amount: -50**
    *   **Issue:** Invalid value (Context-dependent).
```

```text

prompt=What does this Python code do: [x**2 for x in range(5)]?

assistant=starting
assistant_turn_1=This Python code creates a **list of squared numbers** from 0 to 4 using a list comprehension. Here's a breakdown:

- `range(5)` generates the sequence: `0, 1, 2, 3, 4`
- `x**2` squares each number (`x` to the power of 2)
- The square brackets `[...]` collect all the results into a new list

**Step-by-step:**
- `0**2` → `0`
- `1**2` → `1`
- `2**2` → `4`
- `3**2` → `9`
- `4**2` → `16`

**Final output:** `[0, 1, 4, 9, 16]`

List comprehensions like this are a Pythonic, concise way to transform or filter data into lists.

2.  **Identify Key Components:**
   - `[...]` - List comprehension syntax, creates a list.
   - `x**2` - Squares the value of `x`.
   - `for x in range(5)` - Iterates `x` over the numbers 0, 1, 2, 3, 4.

3.  **Determine Output/Behavior:**
   - `range(5)` generates: 0, 1, 2, 3, 4
   - Squaring each: 0²=0, 1²=1, 2²=4, 3²=9, 4²=16

   - The results are collected into a new list.

   - Is the explanation clear? Yes.

   Ready to output. All steps check out.✅

final_answer=This Python code creates a **list of squared numbers** from 0 to 4 using a list comprehension. Here's a breakdown:

- `range(5)` generates the sequence: `0, 1, 2, 3, 4`
- `x**2` squares each number (`x` to the power of 2)
- The square brackets `[...]` collect all the results into a new list

**Step-by-step:**
- `0**2` → `0`
- `1**2` → `1`

- `3**2` → `9`
```

```text
* **Use the built-in "Junk" or "Spam" folder:** Most email services (Gmail, Outlook, Yahoo, etc.) automatically filter spam here. You can usually select all messages in that folder and delete them at once.
* **Mark as spam:** If you see legitimate-looking spam in your main inbox, marking it as spam helps train your email provider's filters to catch similar messages in the future.
* **Unsubscribe:** For newsletters or promotional emails you no longer want, look for the "Unsubscribe" link at the bottom of the email to stop them from arriving in the first place.
```

```text
assistant_turn_2=Here's a draft email for Sarah about the deadline extension:

This extension should provide additional time to [brief reason or benefit, e.g., "ensure quality deliverables" or "address any outstanding items"].

Please let me know if you have any questions or concerns regarding this change.

Best regards,  
[Your Name]

---

Please review this draft and let me know if you'd like me to:
- Add or modify any details (project name, new deadline date, etc.)
- Change the tone or content
- Send it as is

Just provide the specific details you'd like included, and I can update the draft accordingly.

assistant_turn_3=Understood! I won't send the email. The draft is saved here for your reference if you decide to use it later. Just let me know if you need any changes or if you'd like to send it at a different time.
assistant_reasoning_3=The user has decided not to send the email and wants to cancel the action. I should acknowledge this and confirm that no email will be sent. I don't need to use any tools for this.
```

### TC-74

```text
model=udq8
scenario=TC-74 Stateful Multi-Turn Corrections
prompt=Schedule a Team Sync for next Tuesday at 10am, 30 minutes, with Mark.

assistant=starting
assistant_turn_1=[tool_calls_only]
assistant_reasoning_1=The user wants to schedule a Team Sync meeting.
Let's break down the requirements:
- Title: Team Sync
- Date: next Tuesday
- Time: 10am
- Duration: 30 minutes
- Attendee: Mark

First, I need to figure out what "next Tuesday" means.
Today is 2026-03-20 (Friday).
Next Tuesday would be 2026-03-24.
Wait, let's verify:
March 20 is Friday.
March 21 is Saturday.
March 22 is Sunday.
March 23 is Monday.
March 24 is Tuesday.
So next Tuesday is 2026-03-24.
```
